ORDER 
New Delhi, the 25th June, 1986 
S . O . 2477 . — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub 
section ( 1 ) of section 5 read with section 6 of the Delhi 
Special Police Establishment Act , 1946 ( 25 of 1946 ) . The 
Contral Government, with the consent of the State of 
Maharashtra , hereby extends the powers and jurisdiction of 
the members of the Delhi Special Police Establishment to the 
whole of the State of Maharashtra for the investigation of 
offences punishable under Section 302 of the Indian Penal 
Code, 1860 (45 of 1860 ) and attempts , abetments and 
conspiracies in relation to or in connection with the said 
offence and any other offence committed in tlie course of 
the same transaction arising out of the same facts in regard 
to the case crime No, 399 / 86 dated the 9th May , 1986 
registered at Police Station Colaba , Bombay about the death 
of Anthony Veera Swamy Tohna @ Kalya Anthony on 9th 
May , 1986 .

S .O . 2495. — M /s. Aasbetex Engineering Industries Ltd ., 
Bombay were granted an import licence No. P / CG /2097307 
dated 7 - 1 - 1985 for import of capital goods machinery for 
the manufacture of Sintored Products as per list attached 
from U . K . / Switzerland for c .i.f . value of Rs, 48 , 34, 500 ( SF 
438136 and Pound 184650) under foreign cxchange loan from 
ICICI. 

2 . The firm has applied for issue of Duplicate Customs 
Purposeg copy of the above mentioned licence on the ground 
that the original customs copy of licenco has been lost with 
out having been registered with any customs authority and 
utilised at all. 

3 . In support of their contention the licencce has Aled an 
affidavit as required in para 86 of chapter IT of Hand Book 
of Import - Export Procedures 1985- 88. I am accordingly satis 
fled that the original customs purposes copy of Import licence 
No . P /CG/ 2097307 dated 7 - 1 - 85 has been lost by the firm 
In exercise of the power conferred under sub - clause 9 ( cc ) 
of the Import Control Order , 1955 dated 7 - 12 - 1955 as amended 
the said original customs purposes copy of import licence 
No. P / CG / 2097307 dated 7 - 1 - 85 issued to M / s. Asotex 
Engineering Industries Ltd . Bombay is herchy cancelled . 

4 . A duplicate Customs Purposes Copy of the said licence 
is being ingued to the firm separately .

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM & NATURAL GAS 

New Delhi, the 23rd June , 1986 
S . O . 2496 . - Whereas it appears to the Central Govednment 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the transport 
of pertoleum from K - 395 10 G . G . S . V in Gujarat State pipe 
line should be laid by the Oil & Natural Gas Commission . 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of Taying 
such pipeline , it is necessary to acquire the right of user in 
the land described in the schedule annexed hereto : 

Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub -section ( 1 ) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
Lali ) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962 ) , the Central Government 
hereby declares its intention to acquire the right of tser 
therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may , 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the said land to the Com 
Petent Authority , Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Construc . 
tion and Maintenance Division , Makarpura Road , Vadodara , 
( 390009) . 


And every person making such an objection shall also state 
specifically whether he wishes to be hear in person or by 
legal practitioner .

का . पा . 2509:---- कि कोचीम गोवी श्रमिक ( रोजगार मा विनियमन ) 
स्कीम , 1959 को पुनः संशोधित करने के लिए गोपी अमिक ( रोजगार 
का विनियमन ) अधिनियम , 1948 ( 1948 का 9 ) की धारा 4, उपधारा 1 
पारा यथाप्रपेक्षित एक कतिपय स्कीम के प्रारूप का प्रकाशन भारत 
सरकार , परिवहन मंत्रालय , जल -भूतल परिवहन विभाग की अधिसूचना 
संख्या का . प्रा . 612, दिनांक 21 जनवरी , 1988 को भारत के राजपत्र 
के भाग II , षंड 3, उपखंड (ii ) में हुपा था जिसमें उक्त पिना 
के सरकारी राजपक्ष में प्रकाशन की तारीख से 60 दिनों की प्रवधि की 
समाप्ति तक उन सभी व्यक्तियों से प्रापत्तियां और सुझावमामंत्रित किए 
गए थे, जिनके उसमें प्रभावित होने की संभावना थी ; 

और चूंकि उक्त राजपत जनसाधारण फो 17 फरवरी, 1988 को 
सपलब्ध करा दिया गया था ; 


मौर कि जन साधारण से उमत प्रारूप पर कोई पापत्ति या सुभाष 
प्राप्त नही हुए है । 

इसलिए, प्रब केन्द्रीय सरकार उमत अधिनियम की धारा 4, उपचार 
1 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, कोचीन गोदी श्रमिक ( रोजगार 
का विनियमन ) स्कीम, 1959 को पुमः संशोधित करने हेतु निम्नलिखित 
स्कीम बनाती है, प्रति : 
___ 1. (i ) इस स्कीम का नाम कोपीन गोवी अमिक ( रोजगार का 

विनियमन ) संशोधन स्कीम , 1986 है । 
( ii ) यह सरकारी राजपत्र में प्रकाशन की तारीख से प्रवृत्त होगी । 

2. कोचीन गोदी अमिक ( रोजगार का विनियमन ) स्कीम , 1930

AWARD 
Thc Govt. of India , Ministry of Labour in oxerciss of tho 
powers conferred on them under Section 10 ( 1 ) (d ) of the 
I.D . Act., 1947 has roferred the following disputo to this 
Tribunal for adjudication , vide their Order No. L · 12012 /276 
84 -D , II ( A ), dated, the 8th July, 1985. 

SCHEDULE 
" Whether the action of the management of Allahabad 

Bank in relation to their Patna Branch in Imposing 
the penalty of stoppage of two increments of Shri 
Ranbir Singh Saini, Clerk Patna Branch is justified ? 
If not, to what relief is the workinan concerned 

entitled ? " 
The case of the workman is that ibe concerned workman 
Shri R . S . Şaini was working as a Clerk in the Pato Branch 
of Allahabad Bank . He was issued with a mncmo on 3 - 10 - 79 
alleging that on 30 - 7 - 79 at about 11.00 A . M . tho concerned 
workman along with some other employees of tho Allahabad 
Bank had ghoraocd Shri L . D . Bajpei the then Regional Mana 
ger , Allahabad Region , Allahabad in the premises of the Bank 
27 14 , India Exchange Place , Calcutta when Shri Rajpci had 
gone to Calcutta for some official work . The concerned work 
mon filed his reply to the said memo on 17- 12 - 79 stating 
that on the alleged dato ot occurence i.e . on 30 - 7 - 79 he was 
on leave for some urgent work . Ho had also alleged in his 
reply that the memo was served on him with a malafide in 
tention . The records of the Allahabad Bank at Patna will 
show that the petitioner was on leave duly greſted by tho 
authoritics on 30 - 7 - 79 as his wife was in a serious condition 
in the night of between 29 /30 July due to abortion and tho 
concerned workmap was looking after her . After the receipt 
of the reply dated 17 - 12 - 79 the management issved charge 
shoot under the signature of Personnel Manager , Head Offict 
Calccutta vide letter dated 26 - 12 -79 . The samo allegation 
had been repoated in the chargesheet as contained in the memo 
earlier issued to the concerned workman and to which the 
concerned workman had Aled his showcause . The concerned 
workman filed his reply to the chargeshect on 23 - 1 - 80 con 
firming the statement made in his reply to the memo dated 
3 - 10 - 79 , After the submission of the reply by tho workman,

The management did not examine any witaets from Patna 
Branch of Allahabad Bank to show that tho wife of the 
concerned workman had not suffered from Abortion in the 
night of 29 /30 - 7 - 79 , or that the concerned workman WA 
absent from the Bank from 28 - 7 - 79 and that the concerned 
workman had not applied for leave by flling his petition 
for leave in thc office personally on 30 - 7 - 79 . The more re 
petition of fact by 2 or 3 witnesses is not enough to est 
ablish the allegation against a person . The defence of the 
workman has also to be considered and evidence had to be 
led in order to show that the deforce taken by the pro 
ceeding is without any substance . The management, in my 
opinion , has failed to consider the defence which had been 
raised by the concerned workman from the very inception 
of the allegation made against the concerned workrnan , As 
the defence of the concerned workman has not been plo . 
perly dealt with by the cnquiry officer and tho manage 
Dent. I hold that the moro support of the three witnesses 
in the circumstancos of the case is not onough to establish 
the charge .

2 . The facts of the case may be briefly stated . Shri Manilal 
Somabhai Oza (" thc workman concerned ” to be brief) worked 
as e Peon in the Mehsana Branch of the Bank of Baroda 
( the Bank to be brief ) . He was a permanent workmun , On 
29 - 1 - 1979 , an order torminating his services with immediate 
offect by payment of 3 months pay and allowance , in lieu 
of notice was served on him , and his services were accordingly 
terminated . No enquiry whatsoever was, admittedly , held , Tho 
Cast of tho Bank is that the termination of the services of 
tho workman concorned was by way of discharge simpliciter 
for losing confidence in him and sono enquiry was called 
for, Shri. T. R . Misbra , the learned advocate for the workman 
concorned , has however contended that this is not at all , 
caso of discharge simplicitor . According to Shri Mishra , tho 
action has been taken with ultorior motive and it is a colour 
ablo cxorcise of the management s power inasmuch as the 
workmar concerned bas been condemned upheard in flagarant 
violation of the principles of natural justice . It is also con 
tended ( in the statement of claim ) that tho impugned action 
has beon taken to vicitimisc thc workman concerned due to 
inter-union rivalry .

The statement then proceeds to give details of various oiher

Clause (6 ) mentioned above refers to contemplated closing 

down or retronchment of more than $ employees which is

per the directions in the Sastry Award, In cases not involving

that has first to be Reep in the present case in whether this is 
a case involving any disciplinary action for any misconduct 
or jg a case of discharge simpliciter, 


4 . It is by now well settled that the words used in the 
order of discharge and the form it may have taken are not 
conclusive in the matter and the industrial tribunal has to go 
behind the words and form and to decide whether the dis 
charge is actually simpliciter or not. The Tribunal has the 
power and also the duty to X - ray the order and discover its 
true nature. The Tribunal has to ensure that the employer 
has not resorted to camouflage by garbing or cloaking a puni 
tive discharge in the innocuong word ; of discharge simpliciter. 
In the light of this settled legal position , we may now con 
sider the nature of the impugned action . Scrutinising the record 
of the case for this purposo, it is seen that in its wsitien 
statement, the Bank itself has alloged us under 


5. However, according to the Bunk, cven if the impugned 
action is treated as a punitive action , no enquiry was necessary 
inasmuch as the action was taken on the basis of the workman 
admitting his guilt . By now , it is a well settled position in 
law that if the delinquent has admitted his guilt, there is 
no necessity of an enquiry being hold . However, in the pre 
sent case , even otherwise , the guilt appears to have been 
proved before this Tribunal. The workman , in support of 
his case , as revcaled from his statement of clain , has examined 
himself and he has been cross - examined op bchalf of the 
Bank. The Bank has also in rebuttal examined the then Branch 
Manager of the Bank who was also cross -cxamined on behalf 
of tho workman concerned . In his examination -in -chief, the 
workman concerned has tried to explain that the money

cross - examination , he has admitted that he did not pay up

their telephone will be disconnected . He has further admitted 
that even after receiving this call by tho Manager of the bank 
from the Telerhone Department , he had paid up thc money

" 11A . Whero an industrial dispute relating to the dis

6 . The above evidence shows beyond doubt that the work 
man concerned was keeping with himself the Bank s money

Reading the above section , it appears that there are impli 
cations of this Section , which has been inserted by the 
amendment, that the industrial and labour foruma can formu 
late the relief by granting re -instateinent or even after find 
ing that the discharge or retrenchmont was unjust, not grant 
ing it . Similarly , it can grant buck wages partially or 
wholly . Thus, after the insertion of Sec , 11A , the industrial 
Tribunal, if it finds that the order passed is rather harsh , 
it has a power to pass any other appropriatç order as it 
deems fit having regard to the facts and circumstances of 
the case. As observed above , I am of the view that the 
charges levelled against the workman concerned have been 
amply proved from the oral as well as documentary evidenco 
before this Tribunal and therefore , normally the order passed 
should not be interfered with . But, as stated above, tho 
order of discharge without adequate compensation when the 
workman concerned has put in a service of about 9 years, 
is rather harsh and therefore I woulil like to exercise my 
powers under Sec . 11A . However , on consideration of the 
evidence as a whole , I do not think tbis is a fit case for 
reinstatement but the ends of Justice would be met if the 
workman concerned is awarded further compensation , in

alrçady offered to him , equel to his 15 months wages (i.c . 
pay and allowances) which he received at the time of his

7. However, it was argued by Shri Misra for the workman

" In this far- fetched hierarchical relationship . logs of 

confiden “ e if it is to be considered a relevant factor , 
it would have hardly impressed us ." 


However , in the prerent case the workman concerned was a 
peon working in a Bank and the work entrusted to him was

piinn an allowance for this outdcor duty . Now , as the evidence

8 . For the foregoing reasons , the Bank is directed lo pay

The Post of Waterman at Vijayawada - 1 Branch of the 
Bank being a permanent one and the permanent incumhent 
having been promoted and transferred in the year 1979 , those 
appointed to that post do not fit into the definition of " tem 
porary employee " es defined by the Desai Aword . So the

wada- 1 Branch of the Bank are entitled to be continued , Tho 
action of the Bank in hiring and firing workmen in that post 
at will and pleasure is an unfair labour practice. The termina 
tion of Golla Vijayendra Rao , from 28 -6 - 1982 was not on 
account of any disciplinary action against him ; or volumary 
retirement ; or ill -health ctc . Therefore the petitioner union 
submits that the termination of the services of the workman 
Is " retrenchment" within the meaning of Section 2 : 00 ) of the

The Government of India , Ministry of Labour & Rehabili 
tation by its Order No. L- 120121187183 /D .II.A , dated

, The management did not admit the avermeot mentioned in the 
petition . The workmen is not entitled for any relief as 
prayed for his servicos were engaged only tor 70 days as 
a tomporary waterman and after the period was over his 
service was terminated . He has not put in continuous 
service as defined under the 1. D . Act. The temporary em 
ployee has no right to insist that he should be considered 
for further employment. If such a reference is to be acce 
pted all the temporary employecs will have to be appointed 
on permanent basis. It is open to the management to go 
to the open market and select the best persons available. 
There is also nothing like engaging a peraon for a parti 
cular brajich . If all the persons who had woked temporarily 
At some time or the other are to be taken permancntly , 
then the Bank will be over staffed .

of the I, D . Act aro enacted in Chapter V A by an Amend . 
ment Act 1956 once a person claiming protection is u work . 
man within the meaning of Section 2 ( s) of the 1. D . Act 
and he 18 a citizen of india and employed in an industrial 
osnabbthinent and when there is no agreement between 
the employer and workman contrary to procedure of last 
comc lust go , the employee comes under the protection of 
Section 25 - 6 . Similarly under Section 23- H of the I. D . 
Act the workmen should have been retrenched prior to re 
employment and he should have been retrenched from tho 
Game cattgory of service . 

Now the short point to be considered as whether the ter 
mination of G . Vijayender Rao which is in violation of 
Section 25 - G and 25 - H amounts to retrenchinent or not ? 
In S . K . Chatterjec v , Dt. Signal Tele Commn . Engineer 
( 1970 ( II ) LLJ, pago 1791. It was laid down that retrench 
mont of workmen by the Railway which is a Public Utility 
Service was illegal for non -observations of the provisions 
of Section 25- G . It was a case where their Lordship pointed 
out that the Kuilway did got even maintain a seniority ligt 
as provided for Rule 77 of the Industrial Disputes (Central) 
Rules . In Western India Match Factory , Tiruvottiyir V . 
Their Workmen ( 1955 ( II) LLJ , page 628 ] while holding the 
principles of retrenchnçat of last come first go when a 
factory is having various departments and when once such 
department is closed and when the Management refused to 
take back retrenched Workmen and when the work was 
available and began to recruit fresh evidence the Court 
held that the said retrenched workman should be reinstated 
with back wages and said discharge of the workmen is not 
Justified . In Mohanlal v . Bharat Electronic Limited ( 1981 
(II ) LLJ page 70 ) the Supreme Court held while considering! 
Section 2 (00 ) with reference to the facts and circumstancos 
of the casc whether the said termination of service amounts 
to retrenchment or not . It was held that the termination 
would constitute retrenchment and by a catina of decision 
it was well settled that where pre - requisite for valid retrench 
ment as laid down in Section 25 - F had not been complied 
with retrenchment bringing about termination of ab initio 
void . In that case one Mohan Lal who was cmployed in 
Bharat Electronics Limited as Salomón on Sales Depot at a 
salary of Rs. 540 .00 from 8th December 1973 was abruptly 
terminated by lctter dated 12th October 1974 with effect from 
19th October 1974 and the Supreme Court after discussing 
the entire gamut of industrial law held that this amounted 
to retrenchment and it is also a case where the person was 
in continuous service as defined under Section 25- B as in the 
instant case . Now tho Management wanted to take ad 
vantage of the Circulars Exs . W - 4 , W - 5 and W -6 and as a 
matter of policy on the basis of superior , instructions they 
wanted to terminate the staff though he is acting in a perma 
pent post styling him as if he is in temporary vacancy of a 
permanent workman which is not correct. Infact the evi 
dence of MW - 1 would show that S . Nagbhushanam Rao , 
Labipet Branch worked on 3 / 4 wagos and he way posted 
in Vijayawada Branch A9 full time wages as Waterboy . So 
Nagbhushanam was appointed in July 1983 as per Ex. M - 1 
while Vijayender Rao services were terminated on 27 - 6 - 1982 . 
If so how Nagbhushanam Rao was appointed as full limo 
Waterboy at Vijayawada Branch more than ono yoar later . 
Nagbhushanam appointment was not in the vacancy of 
Vijayender Rao or for that matter in the vacancy of Vijaya 
wada I Branch . Nagbhushaoam was appointed for the 
Labipet Branch as per the Managements evidence. He was 
transferred to Vijayawada Branch I to defeat the claim for 
Vljayender Rao for absorption and regularisation . Of course 
the evidence of Workers WW - 1 and WW - 2 would show that 
Nagbhushanam Rao was utilised as Messenger but not as 
a Waterboy, and one of the temporary employees was appoi 
ted as a Messenger was availed for waterboy duties. Evl 
donce of WW - 2 is very clear on this aspect.. Moreover 
Nagbhushanam was transferred to Vijayawada ] Branch 
during the pendency of the disputo to make it appear that 
the vacancy is filled up by pormenont employco . 


that purposo by getting rid of the employee already in 
service , it is absurd . Therefore the termination is unjusti 
tied and post facto justitication is equally unjustified and 
mala fide. In Muller and Phipps India v . Their Employees 
Union ( 1967 ( 11 ) LW , page 222 ] It was held that the argu 
ment of the Managemcat that the cause of retrenched em 
ployce for reemployment under Section 25 - H could not 
espouse by his fellow workmen and that the rcticnched 
workmen cunnot be cither dispute for violation of Section 
25- H . On the face of it arc untenable . If the Manage 
ment is given discretion to make promotion from lower 
cadres to vacancies which should under Section 25- H be 
uled by the retrenched employee. The whole purpo9c of 
Section 25- H would be foiled . Lastly it is the case of the 
management as expressed by MW - 1 that Bank is not required 
to coinply with I. D . Act with respect to G . Vijayender Rao 
as he was appointed purcly on temporary basis. First of 
all the definition of Workmen under Section 2 ( s ) of the 
1. D . Act includes temporary employees, Tho Sastry Award 
and Desai Award also treat the temporary employee as 
workman . According to the Bank the retrenchment arises 
only in the case of those who completed 240 days service in 
a calender year . But the definition of retrenchment as 
found under Section 2 ( 00) of the 1. D . Act does not dis 
tinguish or discriminate between permanent and temporary 
workinen nor does it specify that it applics only to workmen 
who have put in continuous service of 240 days and more . 
A careful perusal of Section 2 ( 00 ) of the I, D , Act would 
show the same. The termination of G , Vijayender Rao 
from 28 -6 - 1982 was not on account of any disciplinary 
uction or voluntary retirement or roaching the age of 
superannuation ; or ill-health . Hence the termination of 
G . Vijaycoder Rao is nothing but retrenchment, within the 
meaning of Section 2 ( 00 ) of the I. D Act. In Kamlesh 
Kumar Rajapikant Mehta v , The Presiding Oficer ( 1980 
(I ) LLJ, page 336 ) The expression " for any rcasons whatso 
cver " " even though seemingly wide" must necessarily draw 
within it s ambit, not any act of commission and omission on 
the part of the workmen but the concept of termination of 
surplus workers service due to reasons such as economy, 
rationalisation in industry and installation of new labour 
saving machinery or devices , standardisation or improve 
ment of plant or technique and the like . So in no case 
there can be any retrenchment except as an imbidcd in defl 
nition of Section 2 ( 00 ) and any termination of service except 
those have beon imbided in the definition amounts to rct 
renchment. In Anakappalla Cooperative Agriculture and 
Industrial Society v . Its workmen 11962 ( II) LL , page 621] 
and in [1957 (I) LLI, 243 ) can be looked into for the said 
proposition . 

Now under Seotion 25- F of the I. D . Act if a person 
puts continuous servico of 240 days and more hc should be 
given one months notice or notice pay compensation AS 
specified. In the instant case since he has not put in 240 
days continuous service Section 25- F did not apply but the 
workman who is posted in a permanent post but cntitled to 
the benefit of protection of Sections 25 - 6 and 25- H relating 
to last come first go and re - employment. In Management, 
W . B . India Ltd ., v . Jagannath (AIR 1974 S . C . Page 1166 ) 
it was laid down even if the service of a temporary employco 
are terminated on the ground of surplus labour is entitled 
to claim retrenchment compensation and it was a caso 
here it was held there was no distinction between badli 
workers and that of a temporary employee. In the instant 
case G . Vijayender Rao who was posted in a permanent 
existing vacancy was not allowed to do 240 days continuous 
service by virtue of Exs, W - 4 , W - 5 and W - 6 atleast he is 
entitled for 14 davg potice under para 522 ( 4 ) of Sastry 
Award as a condition precedent for termination even if 
he did not completo 240 days ; Sastry Award 522 ( 4 ) roads 
as follows : 
" The servico of any employeo other than a permanent 

employco or probationor may he torminated and 
He may leave servico , aftor 14 days notico . If such 
employou loves without giving loavo tuch notice 
he shall bollablo for a week . pay including all 
allowancos. . 


The Managoment dama forward with tho theory of a grco

So the caso of G . Vijayonder Rao he was torminated hy

की धारा 17 के प्रस्मरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार सिंगरणी कोलरीज कम्पनी 
लिमिटेड , गोदावरी बानी , मिमा करीमनगर ( भान प्रदेश ) के प्रबंधतंत्र 
से सम्पद नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच प्रमुबंध में निर्दिष्ट 
भौद्योगिक विवाद में औद्योगिक प्राधिकरण , हैदराबाद के पंचाट को प्रकाशित 
at & 7 bra # SAT 25 JT, 1986 #TheToTTI 


Director daled 5 - 1 - 76 has not made any leference to the 
previous records of this workinaa . In iho absone of su . b 
materials the contention of the I Party that he had put up 
15 years unblemished service should be accepted without 
any reservations . 

12 . The learned counsel for the II Party has submitted 
that though the article found in the possession of the 
workman was found less in value, this will be donc little 
by little until they will be caugbt. There is no merit 
in the submission of the learned counsct as there is no 
material to accept this contention as the courts can not 
relay On surmises and conjunctu . es. 

13 . With regard to the submission of the learned counsel 
for the ll Party that the possess .on of gold bearing quarts 
which amounted to theft though the quantity is very little 
amounts to moral turpitude is without any substance. 

14 . The term moral turpitude was subject to judicial 
scrutiny in various decisions reported from time 10 t me 
and a single judge of the Allahabad High Court Judge 
Privastava , J., in A . I. R . 1963 Allahabad page 527 af er 
considering all the decisions has observed : 
" With great respect, it appears to me that some of the 

observations made in these decisions have been too 
widely stated that if followed literally may make 
every act punishablo in law an offence involving 
moral turpitude. That, however could not be the 
intention with which those observations were made . 
From consideration of the dictionary meaning of 
the words moral and turpitudo as well as the 
repl ratio decidendi of the cases the principle which 
emerges appears to be that the question whether a 
certain offence involves moral turpitude or not will 
necessarily depend on the circumstances in which 
the offence is committed . It is not every punishable 
act that can be considered to be an offence invol 
ving moral turpitude. Had that been so , the quali 
fication involving moral turpitude would not have 
been used by the Legislature and it would have 
disqualified every person who had been convicted 
of any offence. The tests which should ordinarily 
be applied for judging whether a certain offence 
docs or does not involve moral turpitude appear 10 
be : 
( 1 ) Whether the act leading to a conviction was such 

As could shock the moral conscience of society 
in general, ( 2 ) Whether the motive which led to 
the act was a base one and (3 ) whether on account 
of the act having been committed the verpetrator 
could be considered to be of a dopraved charac er 
or a person who was to be looked down upon 

by the society . 
No absolute standard can be laid down for deciding 

whcther a particular act is to be considered one 
involving moral turpitude but the above are the 
general tests which should he annlicd and which 
should in most cases be sufficient for enabling ono 

to arrive at a correct conclusion on the question ." 
13 . The above observations have been relaid by His 
Lordships , P . P . Bopanna while deciding the question of for 
feiting all rights and privileges accruing as a result of nast 
service in a case filed by the legal representative of one Mr. 
Arumugam in writ petition No . 13303 of 1980 . His lordships 
held : 
" That the appellate Authority merely relaying on the 

dictionary meaning of the words moral turpitude 
and the definition of the offence theft in the Todan 
Penal Code camo to a wrong conolusion of jurisdic 
tion facts and therefore its order is liable to be 

quashed , " 
16 . Taking into consideration the observations stated at 
fupra and the facts and circumstances of this case the order 
of dismissal passed by the IJ Party is harsh and disputo to 
the offence alleged to have been committed by the work 
man . 

17 . Now a question arises as to the nature of relief that 
has to be given to the workmon . It is most unfortunate to 
note that the respective counsels have taken a duration of 
10 years to give a finding on this reference and the fault 
also lies on this Tribunal bv granting several adjournirents 
without annlving its mind. Now this Tribunil also preven 
ted by substituting lesser punishment then what was awarded

(a ) It is mentioned that the Union subnitted a number of 
demands with regard to service conditions of the workmon 
cosployed in the Singareni Collierles Company ay tbere was 
no response from the Management , the Union issued a striko 
notice on 30 - 10 - 1982 and it is & Public Utility Service, the 
matter was admitted in the Conciliation by the Assistant 
Labour Commissioner, Central and conciliation meeting was 
held , The Union submitted 15 demands to the management 
and conciliation meetings were also held in respect of thoso 
demonds , and only three demands were referred to tho Tri 
bunal by way of reference . Therefore the Union filed claim 
statement in respect of three demands while other demande 
are pending for reference for being referred . 

(b ) The machine mining operators, machine mining her 
sers and A . M , 50 operators are required to undertake heavy 
manual work and they are exposed to dust and other climatic 
pollutions. The drivers in heavy and light vechicles , Power 
House Workers, Workshop workers are provided with uni 
forms. The Machine Mining operators , Machine Mining 
helpers and AM , 50 operators are in no way inferior to 
that of the staff mentioned above . These workers aro not 
given uniforms with the result their personal uniformis 
being destroyed and they are required to repiace by incurt 
ing heavy financial expenses. The Uniform is a must to 
the workmen and it is being provided in similarly placed 
industries in other states. Therefore the Union demanded 
four pairs of uniforms every year to the Machine Mining 
Operators , Machine Mining Helpers and AM , 50 Operators. 
It is just and reasonable . 

( c ) Washing allowance is connected to the demand of unf 
forms be to be provided . The uniform should be kept and 
maintained in proper manger in order to keep up the uni 
form clean. The cost of washing detergents has been increast 
ing cvery day . Thereforc it is just and necessary that the 
Tribunal be pleased to direct the management to grant 
Washing Allowance of Rs. 25 . 00 per month ner head to 
Machine Mining Operators , Machine Miding Helpers and 
A . M . 50 Operators . 

( d ) The workmen under reference are exposed to heavy 
machinery dust since they have to work in highly polluted 
atmosphere . The workmen by virtue of their heing em 
ployed on such mining Works, have to nocessarily inhale the 
dust and other poisonuovi air which is relcased during the 
mining operations. Therefore the workmen have to protect 
their health by taking medicines and nourishments to arrest 
The ill - effects of the dust which is spread over the area of 
their workspot. Due to this dust number of workers are 
falling gick frequently . The workers in C .S . P , and C . H . P . 
and drivers who are also on coas transport wing are being 
paid special allowance to defray expenses for being exposed 
to heavy dust. Therefore the Unior prayed that tho 
Manageinent he directed to pay dust allowance of Rs. 75 . 00 
rer month per head to the workmen concerned . 

3 . The Management on the other and filed a counter , 
Most of the issues referred to this Tribunal have already 
flower in their demand presented by the Union including 
A . P . Colliery Mazdoor Sangh over which a comprehensive

coinmitment during the period of the agrcement ie, upto

notice the Union is therefore estonned from agitating the 
demand involving monetary commitment during the tenuro 
of the settlement which culminated into a reference : Thero 
is no justification to refer to any of these isples for adjudi 
cation during the tenure of the settlement under Section 
12 ( 31 of the 1. 1 . Act 

( n ) in Coal Mining Industry most of the workers are 
employed underground for extracting cool and they are

(b ) The workers in some of the Surface Departments are 
getting uniforms by virtue of law , customs and practice which 
has come to stay . The comparison between the motor 
Drivers in the matter of uniforms is therefore not correct, 
As per the Settlement dated 29- 1 - 1981 there is no issuo of 
any grant of uniforms to any of the underground workers 
much less machine mining operators , machine mining helpers 
and A . M . 50 workers. Therefore they are not entitled for 
the Uniforms. 

(c ) The payment of Washing allowance is directly con 
nected with the provision of uniforms. When the workers 
are not cntitled for uniforms the question of payment of 
washing allowance does not arise . 


( d ) In some form other most of the workers cmolpyed in 
the coal industry are exposed to dust. Paynxent of dust 
allowance is not in voglic in the coal jadustry . All the 
Unions including the A . P . C . M . Sangh which is a party to 
the present dispute demanded payment of dust allowanco 
to , the present Machine Mining staff etc ., in their strike 
notices in 1980 over which a comprehensive Memorandum 
of Settlement was arrived at on 29 - 1 - 1981 and the union 
did not press the demand for obvious fcagons as no such 
allowance is admissible in the rest of the Coal Industry . 
The dust aitowance is one of the issues figured in the National 
Coal Wage Agreements recently concluded at the National 
level. It was specifically agreed at nara 5 - 3 - 4 of N . C . W . A . 
TIT that the workmen Exposed to heavy dust conditions at 
the place ww be provided a dust mask within six months. 
Thie Settlerrent id aplicable to tho entire coal industry 
including Singgrent and the A . P . C . M . Sangh which claims 
affiliation Lo T. N . T . U . C , as it is a party to the said agree 
ment, The Manacement has already provided regniratory 
filters at consislerable cost to the workmen who are marhine 
mining as against the ordinary masks and the Managemept 
has complied with the settlement much hefore the Settlement 
hrisrome into force . Thus all the demands are untenable 
101 1hey are no justification in any way for making such 
demands.

New Delhi, the 30th June, 1986 
S . O . 2532 . Whercas the Central Government is satisfled 
that the public interest requires that the services in the indus 
lly engaged in the manufacture or production of mineral oil 
( crude ) motor and aviation spirit, diesel oil, Lerosene oil , fuel 
oil, diverse hyddocarbon oils and their blends including synthe. 
lic fuels Lubricating oils and the like, which are covered by 
entry 26 in the First Schedule to the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) , should be declared to be a public utility 
service for the purpose of the said Act ; 

Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub 
section ( vi) of clause ( ) of section 2 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act 1947 (14 of 1947 ) , the Central Government hereby declares 
with immediate effect the said industry to be a public utility 
service for the purposes of the said Act for a period of six 
months.
